HAPT is a vocabulary of terms used to describe the conditions under which an impact assessment is, or is not, required under the GDPR and the EU AI Act. It collects the domains, purposes, processing contexts, entities, data categories, locations, technologies and consequences that appear in the Data Protection Impact Assessment (DPIA) lists published by Member State Data Protection Authorities and in Annex III of the AI Act.

Unlike other vocabularies of this kind, HAPT records for every term the source condition it was derived from: a DPIA Required condition, a DPIA Not Required condition, or an Annex III condition. A term can come from more than one source, which makes the overlap between high-risk processing under the GDPR and high-risk AI systems under the AI Act visible at the level of individual terms.

This is a draft. The vocabulary namespace is provisional and the definitions marked † are drafts that have not yet been reviewed.

Introduction

Deciding whether a DPIA (GDPR Art. 35) or a Fundamental Rights Impact Assessment (AI Act Art. 27) is required depends on describing a processing activity or AI system precisely enough to compare it with the conditions set out in law and guidance. Those conditions are expressed in terms such as evaluation of individuals, publicly accessible space, creditworthiness or emotion recognition. HAPT provides those terms as a controlled vocabulary.

HAPT is the vocabulary companion to IMPACTO. IMPACTO models the impact assessment process (necessity, procedure, outcome, notification) whilst HAPT provides the use cases involved in describing the processing and AI systems that the processes apply to. Both build on the Data Privacy Vocabulary (DPV): HAPT terms are placed under DPV parents wherever possible, and where DPV already has a term for an Annex III condition, that term is reused rather than redefined (see ).

HAPT contains 477 terms: 415 derived from the analysis of Member State DPIA lists and from the DPIA Required and DPIA Not Required condition files, and 62 derived from Annex III of the AI Act. A further 86 existing DPV concepts are mapped to Annex III conditions.

Every concept used in the condition files DPIARequiredConditions.ttl and DPIANotRequiredConditions-2.ttl is defined either here or in IMPACTO. Only the two high-level process classes, impacto:eu-gdpr-DPIARequiredProcess and impacto:eu-gdpr-DPIANotRequiredProcess, remain in the IMPACTO namespace; all other concepts in those files use the hapt: namespace. HAPT local names keep the module prefix of the DPV module each term is intended for (for example hapt:dpv-DetectiveAgency, hapt:pd-Skills, hapt:sector-law-CivilLaw), following the IMPACTO convention.

Six concepts that the condition files previously wrote in the IMPACTO namespace already exist in DPV and are reused from DPV directly, so they are not HAPT terms: dpv:DataBreachNotification, dpv:ContractTerminated, risk:RightsExercisePrevented, sector-finance:AccountManagement, sector-health:PatientInformationManagement, dpv:PersonalSpace.

Source Conditions

Each term carries one or more source-condition labels.

Source conditionMeaningTerms
DPIA Required The term was derived from a processing activity that Member State Data Protection Authorities list as requiring a DPIA under GDPR Art. 35(4), or from the EDPB guidelines on DPIA. The Source row lists the Member States whose lists contain the activity (see ).94
DPIA Not Required The term was derived from a processing activity that Member State Data Protection Authorities list as not requiring a DPIA under GDPR Art. 35(5). The Source row gives the CN identifier of each not-required processing activity the term was derived from. 310
Annex III The term was derived from Annex III of the AI Act, which lists the areas in which AI systems are high-risk under Art. 6(2). The Source row gives the Annex III point and recital, and the term is linked to the corresponding DPV eu-aiact:HighRiskAISystem-AnnexIII-* concept. Terms derived from an exception in Annex III are labelled Annex III exclusion.66
Assessment Process The term relates to the assessment process itself (AI Act Art. 27 and 46) rather than to a condition.11
Not recorded No source was recorded for the term in the previous version of the IMPACTO index, and none of its narrower terms has one. 3

Where a term had no source of its own but has narrower terms that do, the source conditions of the narrower terms are shown and the Source row says so.

Key Concepts

HAPT terms are organised into the following categories. Counts show how many terms in each category come from each source condition; a term with more than one source is counted under each.

CategoryDescriptionTermsReq. Not Req.Annex III
DomainsAreas of application or sectors in which processing or the use of an AI system takes place.7115
PurposesPurposes and activities for which personal data are processed or AI systems are used.1831812740
Processing ContextsContexts and conditions within which processing takes place, such as its scale, systematicity or automation.4214270
Entities and SubjectsOrganisations, authorities, professionals and categories of natural persons involved in or affected by processing.842774
Data CategoriesCategories of personal data and other data whose processing is relevant to impact assessment conditions.5718374
LocationsPlaces and spaces in which processing or monitoring takes place.201182
Technologies and CapabilitiesTechnologies, devices and AI capabilities whose use is relevant to impact assessment conditions.211353
Consequences and ImpactsConsequences and impacts on individuals that arise from processing or from the use of an AI system.221508
Legal Grounds, Measures and Assessment TermsLegal grounds, exemptions, measures and assessment-process terms that determine whether, and how, an impact assessment is carried out.412280
